Document 001: -335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场

Source PDF: all report pdf/-335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

亚⻢逊三季度⾃由现⾦流骤降⾄ -335 亿美元，较市场预期 -77 亿美元超出四倍，背后是单季
773 亿资本开⽀与 481 亿新增债务的双重冲击。 AWS 增速虽创五年新⾼达 39.3% ，却仍低于
买⽅预期，利润率反向下滑。四季度运营利润指引 310 亿，全⾯低于华尔街及买⽅预期。核
⼼症结在于： AWS 每赚 1 美元，背后捆绑近 4 美元新增⽀出。
摘要：
亚⻢逊三季度财报⽤⼀个极其惨烈的数字回答了华尔街全年的疑问：单季⾃由现⾦流消耗⾼达
335 亿美元。尽管 AWS 云业务创下近五年最快增速，但失控的资本⽀出（ Capex ）、疲软的四季
度指引以及暴增的债务，彻底击碎了买⽅的乐观预期。
亚⻢逊最新财报显⽰，三季度⾃由现⾦流录得 -335 亿美元，⽽彭博市场⼀致预期仅为 -77 亿美
元，实际结果超出预期四倍以上。 单季资本开⽀飙升⾄ 773 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 43% ；为填补资
⾦缺⼝，公司在该季度新增 481 亿美元⻓期债务。滚动 12 个⽉⾃由现⾦流从上季度的 -76 亿美元

急剧恶化⾄ -424 亿美元。
与此同时，四季度业绩指引令市场失望。营收指引中值为 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的
2442 亿美元；运营利润指引中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，更全⾯低于买
⽅层⾯ 350 亿美元的 " ⼼理关⼝ " 。 ⾼盛在盘后快评中指出，多项核⼼指标相对于市场预期呈
现 " 中性偏弱 " 结果，预计股价将出现初步负⾯反应。
财报公布后，亚⻢逊盘后股价⼀度暴跌 12.5% ⾄ 223 美元附近（最低触及约 214 美元），远超
7.5% 的隐含波动率，抹平了⼆季度以来的全部涨幅，回落⾄今年 4 ⽉初⽔平。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

对于投资者⽽⾔，核⼼逻辑已经发⽣根本性转变：亚⻢逊的股票之所以 “ 涨不动 ” ，是因为 AWS
业务每带来 1 美元的潜在收益，背后都捆绑着远超 1 美元（甚⾄接近 4 美元）的新增⽀出。华尔街
的预期游戏已经失效，现在市场必须直⾯⾼昂的账单。
那个关键数字：单季 -335 亿美元
如果说有⼀个数字概括了亚⻢逊当前的处境，那就是-335 亿美元—— 这是公司三季度单季⾃由
现⾦流。
这⼀数字的背后逻辑极为直⽩：单季经营性现⾦流约 424 亿美元，但净资本开⽀⾼达 759 亿美
元，两者之差即为现⾦⿊洞。⽽就在⼀年前，亚⻢逊的⾃由现⾦流还处于 529 亿美元的历史峰
值。
资本开⽀的膨胀速度同样触⽬惊⼼。三季度⽑资本开⽀达 773 亿美元，环⽐⼆季度的 542 亿美元
增⻓ 43% 。亚⻢逊同时确认，预计 2026 年全年资本开⽀约 2200 亿美元的计划不变。按前三季度
已累计净⽀出约 1720 亿美元计算，四季度资本开⽀将约为 480 亿美元，较刚刚完成的三季度骤降
约 37% 。
这⼀测算令投资者⽣疑：要么亚⻢逊将在四季度⼤幅收紧⽀出，与 CEO Andy Jassy 所描述的 " 需

求持续超过供给 " 形成⽭盾；要么三季度存在⼤量⽀出的提前确认，使得原有指引本⾝已失效。
⽆论哪种情形，市场均难以认同 2027 年资本开⽀将低于 2026 年 —— 此前买⽅预期明年资本开⽀
在 3200 亿⾄ 3750 亿美元之间，⽽亚⻢逊在本次财报中对 2027 年的数字只字未提。
谁来买单？债务飙升，资产负债表急剧恶化

### Page 3

当运营现⾦⽆法覆盖⽀出时，钱只能从别处来。亚⻢逊的资产负债表给出了答案：疯狂发债。
为了⽀撑庞⼤的开⽀，亚⻢逊在三季度筹集了 481 亿美元的新增⻓期债务。今年前九个⽉，公司
新增⻓期借款⾼达 1151 亿美元，导致⻓期债务⼏乎翻了三倍，从 2025 年底的 656 亿美元飙升⾄ 9
⽉底的 1731 亿美元。
与此同时，公司的财务健康度正在全⾯下滑：
现⾦储备从 868 亿美元骤降⾄ 558 亿美元。
利息⽀出同⽐翻了三倍多，从去年同期的 5 亿美元飙升⾄ 17 亿美元。
“ 其他⻓期负债 ” 也⼏乎翻了三倍，从 360 亿美元增⾄ 999 亿美元。
值得注意的是，上述数据还不包括亚⻢逊⾸次发⾏的英镑计价债券，以及⽤于购买英伟达芯⽚
的 80 亿美元特殊⽬的载体安排。
正如市场所观察到的逻辑链条： “债务变为资本开⽀，资本开⽀变为营收，营收变为每股收益。 ”
亚⻢逊本季度在规模上完整展⽰了这条链条的第⼀个环节。
AWS 与核⼼业务：五年最佳增速仍难填买⽅胃⼝
尽管 AWS 云业务表现强劲，营收增⻓ 39.3% ⾄ 460 亿美元，创下近五年最快增速，且 CEO Jassy

给出了 2027 和 2028 年 AWS 增⻓ “ 超过 40%” 的乐观预期。但这对挑剔的买⽅来说还不够：39.3%
的增速虽然击败了华尔街约 37% 的预期，但低于买⽅真正期待的 42% 。
更糟糕的是利润率⾛向了错误的⽅向。AWS 营业利润率从⼆季度的 39.4% 下滑⾄ 38.7%，未能
达到买⽅预期的 “40% 出头 ” 。
其他业务⽅⾯，零售表现平稳（北美销售额增⻓ 6% ，国际增⻓ 8% ，剔除汇率影响）。但⼴告业
务增速放缓⾄ 12%（剔除汇率影响），公司将其归咎于 Prime Day 的 “ 时间异常 ” ，并承诺四季度将
反弹⾄⾼⼗位数增⻓。
此外，每股收益（ EPS ）录得 1.89 美元，不及预期的 1.97 美元。这主要是因为去年三季度包含了
Anthropic 股份带来的 95 亿美元税前收益，⽽今年⾮运营项⽬反⽽造成了 12 亿美元的拖累（包含

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

翻倍的利息账单）。唯⼀符合预期的是营业利润，达到创纪录的 281 亿美元（利润率 13.9% ），精
准命中买⽅传⾔的 280 亿美元。
疲软的四季度指引：全⾯不及预期
四季度业绩指引是此次财报的另⼀个失望来源。
营收指引区间为 2400 亿⾄ 2470 亿美元，中值 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的 2442 亿美
元。运营利润指引为 285 亿⾄ 335 亿美元，中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，
也低于买⽅⼼理关⼝的 300 亿⾄ 350 亿美元区间的两端，缺⼝超过 15 亿美元。
据摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 此前预测，⼀旦亚⻢逊内部 AI 助⼿ Leo 正式上线、相关成本开始
资本化，运营利润将获得约 10 亿美元的额外提振 —— 即便如此，实际指引仍未能达到预期。

⼴告业务是指引中唯⼀的亮点：三季度⼴告增速放缓⾄ 12% （剔除汇率影响），公司将此归因于
Prime Day 的 “ 时间性异常 ” ，并预计四季度将反弹⾄ ” ⾼⼗字头 ” 增速区间。不过，随着亚⻢逊屏
蔽 Meta Muse 等 AI 购物代理、市场担忧 AI 代理购物将侵蚀赞助商品⼴告收⼊，⼴告业务的⻓期前
景仍存争议。
为什么这只股票 “ 永远涨不动 ” ？
摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 在财报前已对亚⻢逊的困境作出精准诊断：
据⾼盛和瑞银的评估，亚⻢逊财报前是科技股中拥挤度最⾼的多头头⼨之⼀，⾼盛 TMT 桌拥挤
度评分 8/10 ，瑞银情绪量表 9/10 。⾼度拥挤的多头持仓意味着，⼀旦现实未能匹配预期，⽌损卖
压将成倍放⼤。
三季度财报的结构性⽭盾清晰可⻅： AWS 加速兑现，但每⼀美元的云业务收⼊，背后跟随着远
超⼀美元的新增⽀出。本季度，这⼀⽐例接近四⽐⼀。  只要这⼀模式未能扭转，股价上涨的逻
辑便始终⾯临来⾃资产负债表的压制。
这张账单是：单季 773 亿美元的资本⽀出、 335 亿美元的现⾦消耗，以及 481 亿美元的新增债

务。亚⻢逊的股票之所以表现挣扎，是因为 AWS 带来的每⼀美元上⾏空间，都捆绑着超过⼀美
元的新增⽀出 —— ⽽最新财报显⽰，这个⽐例接近⼀⽐四。
市场终于意识到，⾼昂的基础设施投⼊正在⽆情地吞噬股东的现⾦流。
“AMZN 是今年最让主流投资者沮丧的股票之⼀ …… 华尔街对 2027 年 AWS 的预测低得可笑 …… 正
因如此，投资者都把它视为容易超预期的故事，但所有⼈都知道这⼀点，这恰恰是它涨不动的核
⼼原因。 ”

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：157.00-159.00

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
歐元兌美元匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈 ， 最高來到1.1269， 終場收在1.1239， 週線上漲42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1300

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.58%， 低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。

顧 • AI安全監管升溫，短期壓抑市場情緒：Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度，並引入獨立第三方
評估等安全機制，引發市場對AI資本支出降溫的疑慮。不過，前沿模型開發速度放緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。
Alphabet、微軟及亞馬遜近期AI相關積壓訂單持續攀升，合計達約1.64兆美元，其中微軟及Alphabet分別突破6,000億及
5,000億美元，搭配雲端業務持續成長，顯示企業AI需求仍具韌性，相關訂單有望逐步轉化為未來數年的營收與獲利。
• 槓桿壓力逐步消化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值：美國雲端巨頭擴大AI資本支出，壓抑短期自由現金流；相較之下，亞洲
科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

法 說 前 瞻              產業：電子零組件 
國巨法說會前財報預估 
預估 4Q26 營運逐步墊高，仍關注未來獲利狀況

| 財報訊息   
國巨即將於 10/29(四)舉行法說會， 3Q26營收季增 11.4%至495.3億
元，符合預期，市場普遍預估毛利率介於38~40%，EPS約4.5元，後
續將關注公司對於2027年產能規劃 、 整體產業供需(同業擴廠狀況及
AI機櫃時程)以及產品價格看法。 
| 營運現況  
3Q26營收符合預期：3Q26營收季增11.4%至495.3億元，主要受惠於
AI相關應用需求，且標準品及特殊品出貨均呈現穩定成長，營收達
成率100%，符合我們先前預期，而獲利部分，隨3Q26將有新合約逐
漸發酵，推測獲利將較上季提升，毛利率和營益率我們預估兩者分
別為39.5%和28.8%，稅後EPS達5.4元。 
| 營運展望  
本季營收預估季增 5%至520億元：我們預估4Q26營收有望季增5%
至520億元 ， 成長動能來自於AI新平台陸續放亮對於元件的需求 、 日
韓大廠的外溢訂單以及產品組合轉佳，且從10月代理商提供的交期
數據觀察，村田及三星電機的MLCC交期已長達約30及52週，趨勢
持續往上，若對照疫情期間的高峰(3Q21村田高容MLCC曾達30~33
週)，顯示供需仍吃緊，而目前擴廠及設備準備時間較長，各廠僅藉
由產線優化及產線轉換提升約10%~15%，因此推測B/B ratio 也保持
在高檔，獲利部分，根據供應鏈調查，部分日韓廠有漲價動作，國
巨有望跟進漲價，因此預估毛利率將延續上升趨勢，提升 0.75個百
分點至40.3%，稅後EPS達5.75元。

### Page 2

PCB CCL upgrading continues: M10 is under qualification, which should start 
shipments from 2H27, while we expect M11 shipments to start no later than 2030  
For the PCB CCL industry overall, the key revenue/profit driver in our view is the solid 
specification upgrading trend (e.g., AI M7 ramp up in 2024, the M8 ramp in 2025, and 
M9 in 2026, Exhibit 3) which continues to drive high-end CCL players’ ASP growth by 
25-50% YoY annually in 2024-2026, and we are seeing the trend continuing and 
accelerating in coming years, despite the concerns of some investors in our 
conversations that a technology migration slowdown is impacting CCL players’ 
revenue/profit growth opportunity.  
During the TPCA show, we saw both EMC and TUC launch their M10/M11 solutions 
at the event, while both companies mentioned they are closely engaging with end 
customers for the next-generation AI R&D projects, based on the M10/11 solution (+ 
under qualification now). We believe EMC should start shipping its M10 products from 
2H27 at the earliest for specific AI customer’s GPU AI rack solutions and that shipments 
should ramp up significantly in 2028 as more GPU and ASIC AI projects start adopting 
this solution. For TUC’s M10 products, we believe the company will start shipment 
from 2028 for not only high-end 3.2T Ethernet switch projects but also for some ASIC 
projects.  
Both EMC and TUC are currently under verification with clients for their M10/M11 grade 
CCL, which incorporates Q-glass. In terms of the possibility of adopting PTFE-based 
CCL, the new solution continues to face production difficulties, making us less 
positive on the solution’s progress, and we only expect the MP (mass production) 
time to occur after 2H28 at the earliest (our supply chain checks suggest the 
production yield for PTFE-based PCB is running at 10-15% vs. average yield of 
80-90% for PCB with M9 grade CCL adopting Q-glass). As a result, we believe the PCB 
upgrade for switch tray in Nvidia’s Rubin Ultra GPU servers starting from 2H27 could 
have a high likelihood of adopting M9/M10 grade CCL with Q-glass/HC (Hydrocarbon) 
instead of PTFE, potentially implying better EMC/TUC share than we currently expect 
over the next 2-3 years).

Exhibit 1: We are seeing more substrate/PCB/CCL players’ 
future investment plans in 2027-30 being supported by 
their end customers

Exhibit 2: Depreciation % of total revenue should continue 
to go down with customers’ investment in equipment or 
more prepayment/subsidy

New opportunity to enter the high ASP/GM ABF substrate supply chain for TW CCL 
supply chain players 
Given the relatively slow capacity investment to date from Japanese substrate material 
suppliers (ABF CCL/ T-glass/ micro thin copper foil etc.industries are mostly dominated 
by Japanese players, with more than 95% market share in the past 10+ years) while 
demand continues to trend up (we are seeing ABF substrates’ material usage doubling 
every 1-2 years, due to the expanding substrate size/ increasing substrate layer counts / 
decreasing substrate production yield rate), we started to see end customers 
qualification processes for Taiwan and mainland China ABF substrate supply chain 
vendors accelerate from 1H26, to better overcome supply constraints, implying a 
long term share gain trend from the Taiwan/mainland China new substrate material 
suppliers (including EMC and Co-Tech in our coverage space). We therefore believe 
EMC likely starts taking ABF substrate CCL market share from 2H26 (TAM could be 
up from US$0.8bn-1.2bn in 2026 to US$2.0bn-2.5bn in 2027 and US$4.0bn-4.5bn in

### Page 4

2028, accounting for ~15%/19%/22% of EMC’s 2026/27/28E revenue, per GSe, 
which not only suggests significant upside potential for EMC’s ASP outlook (ABF 
substrate CCL ASP per full sheet is ~US$1,500 vs. M7/8 only at US$130/180, Exhibit 
5) but should also lead to a solid GM uptrend. We estimate the ABF substrate CCL GM is 
now at 50%+ which could further increase closer to the industry average of ~70%+ 
in the long term based on our industry checks (vs. our 2026/27/28 GM estimate on 
EMC is 35.2%/40.7%/43.4%). Also, we believe that micro-thin copper foil, which is 
dominated by Japan vendors, should also start to see some new suppliers starting from 
late 2027/early 2028, while Co-Tech said that it is preparing R&D resources and is 
working on designs for the new production lines for this new substrate-level product, 
which we estimate could start contributing to the company’s revenue from 2H28 at the 
earliest per GSe.  
Moreover, considering that the ABF substrate size/layer count both continue to increase, 
with less favorable yield rate and production efficiency, we expect the high-end AI ABF 
substrates consumption on ABF CCL to increase significantly. Based on prevailing 
specifications, we believe a quarter sheet of ABF CCL can currently produce ~6-8 units 
of high-end ABF substrates, but that number could decrease significantly to 2-3 units 
per quarter sheet in 2028 based on the substrate design, which should lead to a solid 
unit volume demand uptrend.  
In terms of supply, EMC said that it could have 1.5mn sheets/month capacity by end of 
2027 for ABF substrate CCL during TPCA event, which would then likely account for 50% 
of the global ABF substrate CCL capacity on our estimates, suggesting a strong focus by 
the company to compete in this high ASP/GM product segment.

PCB/CCL upgrades to continue with limited risk from CPO adoption 
We see limited near term risk of Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) adoption as the industry 
should start with switch trays in GPU servers from 2028E, when we estimate switch 
board to account for 14% of a total CCL TAM of US$48bn in 2028E. In considering an 
extreme case where all switch boards are downgraded to M4/M7 grade CCL, we would 
expect the impact to result in a <10% reduction in the 2028E CCL TAM, an impact which 
we believe would be manageable. At the TPCA show, we saw ZDT showcase its 30 layers

HDI PCB switch board, and the company said that it is confident that switch board will

### Page 7

gainers
EMC to see solid upside on profit potential with the new substrate CCL opportunity
We continue to see EMC make progress in the new ABF substrate CCL business, a market
which the company entered in early 2026 and started producing high-end ABF substrate
CCL from 2H26, with monthly revenues reaching NT$150-200mn (<1% of the company’s
Sep monthly revenue) for the most high-end TPU substrate CCL market (see here). We
believe EMC could become one of the market leaders within the next 3-5 years (we are

expecting 20-30% market share in 2028E and trending up significantly afterward), given
its track record of successfully entering new markets over the past two decades.
Furthermore, the company is well-positioned to gain share in the tight supply market as
the company is proactive with its substrate CCL expansion plans, and the company
expects to expand its substrate CCL capacity to 4-5x of current level by 2027 (current
substrate CCL capacity at 300k sheet/month), which should account for 50% of the

global substrate CCL capacity by end of 2027E, implying a good opportunity for the
company to further ramp up market share in the long term.
M10/11 upgrades still the key to earnings growth for EMC & TUC in the next 3+
years, while the favorable S/D outlook should continue to drive the like-for-like
base pricing in coming quarters
We see EMC and TUC being well-positioned in the ongoing CCL upgrade trend as both
companies are currently under verification with clients for their respective next

generation M10/M11 grade CCL and are also future proofing their product-portfolios by
continuing to develop PTFE-based CCL. For the new M10 grade CCL, we expect EMC will
start shipments from 2Q27-3Q27 while TUC should start shipments from 2028, leading
to meaningful upside potential for each company’s revenue, as EMC/TUC’s current ASPs
have been approximately US$63/46 in 2026 while the M10 grade CCL ASP is currently
priced at ~US$800 per sheet, implying substantial ASP upside potential from here, all

else equal.
We continue to see a solid S/D outlook for high-end CCLs in coming years (see here) as
capacity expansion for these CCLs continues to track slower than demand growth (see
here). However, we note TUC revised up its capacity target and expects it to increase
2.5x+ over 2026-30 (8/5/30% upward revision to the year-end target capacity for
2026/27/28 from previous guidance), and based on company comments we believe the
new capacity is fully booked by clients, implying a solid revenue/profit growth outlook.

Co-Tech is currently obtaining LTAs to secure high-end copper foil capacity,
suggesting a solid long term market share and revenue outlook
We believe Co-Tech is well-positioned to benefit from the structural demand for high
end copper foil in a shortage environment, as the company announced capacity
expansion plans for a new Plant 4 in Malaysia, with capacity being secured by more than
one client, per the company. The company now target its annual HVLP3+ effective

capacity to reach 10K/20K/30K tons by end of 2027/28/29 (equivalent of
833/1666/2500 tons/month by 2027/28/29 end vs. current HVLP3+ effective capacity
of ~250 tons/month). This should enable the company to achieve further gains in market
share amid high end copper foil supply shortage environment (see here), as well as
seeing a better pricing environment going forward.
We expect Co-Tech’s new capacity, after ramp up, should continue to see 100% UTR, as

ZDT continues to maintain key one-stop shop status for all ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP 
products, suggesting solid market share outlook in long term 
We remain positive on ZDT as the company continues to make progress with its optical 
module mSAP and AI PCB business, and the company mentioned that both of these 
business are seeing capacity bookings by clients. We believe ZDT will become a market 
share leader in the optical module mSAP industry (see here), as we expect the company 
to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two years based on announced capex 
plans. Most suppliers in the optical module mSAP industry are proactively expanding 
capacity as CPO adoption risk remains limited.  
Also, considering that ZDT is the only company that manufactures all types of 
ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP products, it is well positioned for further share gains not only in 
the optical transceiver market, but also in the AI PCB, ABF, BT markets. Additionally, we 
estimate that these new products will likely carry 35-50%+ GMs in 2027, implying a solid 
revenue/profit outlook for the company going forward.  
We also see other companies within the CCL/IC substrate sector flagging supply 
tightness across the whole supply chain as industry expansion has not been sufficient to 
meet prevailing demand. This is in line with our view that suppliers with timely capacity 
expansion plans are better positioned to make further market share gains and benefit 
from stronger pricing/profitability amid a supply-constrained environment.

### Page 10

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$10,200 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers.

### Page 11

Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,010 is based on a 22x 2027E P/E (+2x 
STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).  
Key downside risks:  (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL segment; 
(2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch shipments 
globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Co-Tech 
Co-Tech is a global leading general server copper foil supplier, with ~60% market share 
in 2025 per GSe. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain opportunities in the 
near term, considering its proactive HVLP capacity expansion plan and strong 
know-how in the advanced copper foil industry. Also, adoption of HVLP4 copper foil 
from AI servers and 1.6T networking switches starting in 2H26 should be a key demand 
driver for Co-Tech, in our view. Additionally, the company’s proactive pricing strategy 
and ability to improve production yield should drive revenue momentum in the long 
term.  
We are Buy rated as we believe Co-Tech will see better revenue/profitability in the long 
term given its shift to a more premium product mix along with proactive capacity plans 
(Plant 3 to be ready by end of 2027) and this should lead to strengthened valuations.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$940 is based on a 22x 2028E P/E, which is 
+1SD above the company’s average upcycle valuation in the past 10-years. 
Key downside risks: (1) earlier than expected CPO adoption in the computing IC, (2) 
slower than expected yield rate improvement speed, and (3) much stronger than 
expected capacity growth from HVLP peers, limiting pricing power

### Page 12

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most

from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which

should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology,

including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle
average valuation).
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Summary: On 29 October, Realtek reported 3Q26 results that missed our estimates but 
were in line with the consensus. This was mainly due to weaker-than-expected revenue 
likely on a deeper demand rebalancing despite pricing power on supply-chain cost 
passthrough. While 4Q26 outlook looks intact, management expects steady topline growth 
into 2027, supported by a multi-year bandwidth upgrade cycle, continued pricing resilience 
and emerging AI opportunities from cloud to edge to physical AI, despite likely still subdued 
PC and consumer electronics demand. We rate the stock Outperform (2) premised upon 
our key thesis of a multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) bandwidth upgrade cycle 
across wireline and wireless to benefit Realtek, which is further echoed by management’s 
2027 outlook.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 results miss on lower topline. Realtek reported 3Q26 net profit of TWD4.1bn, 
missing our estimate by 7% while in line with the consensus. With financial metrics such 
as margins broadly in line, the miss was mainly due to lower-than-expected topline 
which came in 8% below our estimate at TWD37bn. We believe the weak revenue was 
driven by a deeper demand rebalancing after customer restocking and pre-stocking in 
1H26 on seasonality and supply-chain uncertainties, flagged in our note, Downgrading: 
pricing to bridge rebalancing but …, 4 August. Although Realtek raised prices to pass on 
cost inflation to the supply chain, it failed to offset such rebalancing. As usual, 
management gave no quantitative guidance for 4Q26 but expressing “prudent and 
steady” revenue run rates, in line with our expectations. We will be reviewing our model 
in due course.

• Key takeaways. At today’s results call, management further expected a steady topline 
growth heading into 2027, driven by: 1) multiple-G bandwidth upgrade cycles across 
wireline and wireless networking, including Wi-Fi 7 and 8, multi-G Ethernet, PON and 
switch, to enhance product mix with market-share gains adding to upside; 2) likely 
sustained pricing power with the company continuing to pass on inflated costs to the 
supply chain; and 3) despite likely still subdued demand from PC and consumer 
electronics markets, the ongoing development of AI, from cloud to edge AI to physical AI 
applications, is creating growth opportunities and tallying with our investment thesis of 
the multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade cycle to structurally benefit Realtek.

Recommendation 
We rate Realtek Outperform (2). The stock price has generally consolidated around 
TWD670-790 over the past 3 months due to lack of catalysts amid its demand rebalancing, 
suggesting patience for investors. Key downside risk to our call would be any commodity 
price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand rebalancing.

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Win Semi’s 3Q26 core business (operating income) was 49/41% 
higher than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by a more favorable 
product mix with higher contribution from the high margin 
infrastructure business (accounted for 39% of total revenue in 3Q26 
vs. 34% in 2Q26), resulting in a 9.2ppt QoQ increase in 3Q26 GPM, 
and came in 6.9/6.1ppt above GSe/BBG consensus. Win Semi’s 
OPEX increased to NT$775mn (vs. NT$743mn in 2Q26). NI was 
67/110% higher than GSe/BBG consensus due to the high 
non-operating income of NT$698mn (account for 33% of total 
pretax income). 
For 4Q26, the company expects revenue to increase by low single 
digit QoQ, and expects cellular to see QoQ decline due to weak 
season while the remaining businesses (including 
infrastructure/Wi-Fi/optical) will see flattish to small QoQ increase. 
For GM, the company guides the 4Q26 level to maintain at 
high-thirties %, which is much better than both our prior 
expectation and BBG consensus at 31.8/31.4%. Management 
indicated 3Q26 UTR to be higher at 75% (was 65% in 2Q26), and 
expect to maintain at similar level in 4Q26.  
The company continues to highlight the progress with its AI-related 
business, with its new PD (photodiodes) products started to ramp 
up from 2H26, with more contribution expected in 2027 along with 
more PD capacity coming online. For the new LD (laser diodes) 
products, the company continues to work with multiple clients on 
multiple new solutions (including CW laser, EML solutions and 
VCSEL), but expects to see more contribution in 2028 due to the 
lengthy reliability tests (takes 6M+ per our estimates). The company 
reiterated that CAPEX this year is project driven, which will mainly 
focus on expanding InP and GaN capacity for the datacom optical 
and infrastructure business.

Upward revision to PA foundry TAM reflecting foundry continue to gain
share despite weak smartphone shipment
We have revised up our 2026/27/28E P A foundry TAM by 5/4/2% to
US$1.3bn/1.5bn/1.6bn despite smartphone shipment remaining weak (GSe now at
-10%/+2%/+2% in 2026/27/28, was -10%/+3%/+1% previously, see here) as the
smartphone market remains exposed to the rising memory cost environment (see here).
The upward revision is based on our expectation that PA foundry house will continue to

gain share in the smartphone PA segment as newer smartphone projects from design
houses are being allocated to foundries due to the higher production costs and
increasing design complexity of the PA. This will benefit Win Semi in the mid to long term
(Win Semi’s revenue from cellular business already saw 28% YoY YTD). We now expect
the market TAM to deliver an 8% CAGR over 2026-30E, and we expect foundries’ cellular
PA shipments to increase by 11% in 2026 (Exhibit 2), outperforming our 2026

smartphone shipment estimates of -10% YoY due to the higher than expected PA
foundry penetration (Exhibit 5).
Win Semi delivered 3Q26 revenue that is better than the company’s guidance (actual
QoQ at 31% vs. guidance of low-teens QoQ), with the cellular business in peak season.
Management expects this momentum to slow down in 4Q26 and guided revenue to see
QoQ decline in the cellular business. We now expect Win Semi’s PA foundry revenue to
grow 31% YoY in 2026 (was 28% previously), outpacing the overall PA foundry TAM

growth of 22% YoY, as the company continues to focus on mid-to-high-end and
premium smartphone models (iOS camp accounts for 30-40% of the company’s cellular
business per our estimate), which are relatively more resilient to rising memory costs.
Moreover, we expect Win Semi’s PA foundry market share to decline from 74% in 2020
to 57% in 2026, and further lower to ~50% by 2030, but its revenue will continue to
expand further due to the growing foundry TAM.

We continue to expect Taiwan PA foundry players to see modest recovery in 2026
CAPEX. However, we do not view this as an indication of a recovery in demand for the
overall PA foundry industry. Win Semi maintained its 2026 CAPEX guidance of
NT$2-3bn, with the majority of spending allocated to the new InP and GaN capacity
rather than GaAs capacity expansion. We continue to believe GaAs capacity remains
sufficient as we estimate Win Semi’s quarterly utilization rate could reach at most 85%

throughout 2026-28E (Exhibit 1), suggesting no meaningful need for additional GaAs
capacity expansion.

### Page 5

Investment view 
The company has started to see increasing contribution from the new PD product in 
3Q26 but the contribution remains very limited (overall optical business only saw high 
single digit QoQ in 3Q26), while the company expects PD product revenue to further 
ramp up in 2027 along with more PD capacity coming online. This is tracking more slowly 
than our expectation, and we now expect the datacom optical business to account for 
only a mid-single-digit of 2026E revenue (was expecting high-single-digit previously). 
Meanwhile, progress in new high end LD products (including CW lasers, EML solutions 
and VCSEL) remains broadly the same since last earning call, with the company is still at 
verification stage and expect more meaningful contribution in 2028E. As such, we 
believe Win Semi’s revenue remains highly exposed to the potential weakness in 
smartphone demand, as we estimate smartphone related business (including cellular, 
WiFi, and optical) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2026E. The contribution

from datacom optical through 2026-27E could not reach scale, and it remains highly

As a result, we believe Win Semi’s stock price remains too elevated (trading at over 50x
2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG consensus) with limited contribution from the datacom
optical business.
We maintain our conservative view on Win Semi’s stock performance, given (1) the
contribution from datacom optical products (e.g., PDs and LDs) remains limited in

near-term, with the higher margin LD products potentially seeing more contribution in
2028E, (2) the company’s exposure in cellular business remains high (30%+), and the
smartphone market continue to be impacted by high memory cost; and (3) given the
slow technology upgrade speed in the GaAs foundry market, we believe competitors
such as AWSC could catch up with the technology and is better positioned to gain share
given their lower cost structures and more competitive pricing.

Maintain Sell, with a new 12-m TP of NT$162 (from NT$142).

### Page 7

Earnings revisions  
We raise our 2026E revenue by 2% after factor in 3Q26 results and raise our 2027/28E 
revenue by 5/6% to factor in higher infrastructure and optical business contribution. We 
revise up our 2026/27/28E GM estimates by 3.8/2.1/1.6ppt as we expect more margin 
expansion driven by higher UTR and the higher contribution from high margin 
infrastructure business. Overall, we revised up our 2026/27/28E EPS estimates by 
47/6/7%. 
Also, we introduce our 2029E earnings estimates with this report.

Valuation 
We maintain our Sell rating, and revise up our TP from NT$142 to NT$162, based on the 
same target PE of 24x (1x STDV lower than the industry upcycle average valuation), 
factoring in our earnings revisions and, consistent with our group coverage practices, roll 
over our valuation period from 4Q26-3Q27 to 2027E.

Investment Thesis and TP & risks

Win Semi is a key GaAs-based semi foundry player with c.49% global market share as of 
2025. We believe the company’s market share could decline given its much 
higher-than-peer product pricing in the demand downtrend. We also expect the

company to be impacted by the slow PA foundry market growth outlook given weak

optical products, automotive LiDAR opportunity, satellite communication market
opportunity, stronger 5G BTS deployment in EU/US market, datacom optical
opportunity etc.), which should increase its revenue/GM in the long term. However, we

believe Win Semi’s market share loss will continue to weigh on its long-term growth
opportunity. We are Sell-rated given the ongoing PA foundry industry downcycle, along
with our cautious view on the company’s market share outlook. Win Semi is now trading
above its average downcycle P/E multiple. Key upside risks are (1) easing competition in
the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade
trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related business.

Our 12-month target price of NT$162 is based on 24x 2027E EPS, which is 1x STDV
lower than the industry upcycle average valuation since 2016. Key upside risks are (1)
easing competition in the China PA foundry market, (2) faster-than-expected progress
on 5G/WiFi 7 upgrade trend, and (3) faster-than-expected growth from LEO/AI-related
business.

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.

### Page 6

TO P I C  1 | COP31’s UNIQUE STRUCTURE
COP31’s unique dual structure will need to bridge divides among negotiating groups and build consensus
As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations. This dual structure is the result of a compromise after
both countries bid to host. Feedback post pre-COP meetings on the unique arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any disagreement between the two countries

would be handled should it occur. Both countries clearly want this year’s COP to focus on climate finance implementation and clean-energy growth.
A unique leadership structure
COP31 will take place in Antalya, Türkiye. The setup is unusual, emerging as part of a deal
between Türkiye and Australia after both countries bid to host the event. What began as a
compromise could become an advantage, provided the two countries remain aligned. Their
differing diplomatic relationships could help them bridge divides between negotiating groups

and build common ground on climate action and finance.
How it will work
Murat Kurum, Türkiye’s Minister of the Environment, will serve as COP31 President, overseeing
the conference and Action Agenda. Australia’s Minister for Climate Change and Energy, Chris
Bowen, will serve as President of Negotiations, including drafting final texts, with authority to
steer government-to-government talks. Pre-COP preparations have reportedly been positive.
They will, however, both likely face criticism from climate advocates asking for a plan to shift

from fossil fuels, notably coal, given efforts to position themselves as global climate leaders.
The Action Agenda includes targets for electrification, waste, buildings and circularity
This voluntary Agenda proposes 2035 targets of a 35% electricity share of final energy use (up
from 23% currently), halving projected municipal waste growth, cutting building energy
intensity by at least 25% and reaching at least 15% circular material use. While pledges are

non-binding, they can signal policy and decisions that could support infrastructure investment.
Formal negotiations to focus on implementing existing commitments
Formal negotiations seek consensus (or lack of objection) on implementation pathways. The
key need is guidance on how agreed climate-finance commitments will be funded and
delivered, and on countries’ roles. Adaptation will also be a focus, with countries aiming to
reach agreement on how to advance the Global Goal on Adaptation after discussions stalled

earlier this year. These formal talks remain separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Sources: COP31 Action Agenda, IEA and Eco-Business

### Page 7

TO P I C  2 | C L I MATE F I N AN C E
Climate finance delivery will be a central test for COP31
June’s Bonn preparatory talks reportedly made little progress and exposed persistent finance divisions, leaving COP31 to turn both the $300bn and $1.3tn-by-2035 goals into a more credible
delivery plan. This is a big ask and requires clearer public/private roles, consistent tracking mechanisms, more concessional finance, risk-sharing and guarantees that can help reduce the cost of

capital. New large-scale finance commitments appear unlikely, with the EU already stating it will not make new pledges.
7

Context: Developed countries agreed to lead mobilisation of at least
$300bn a year by 2035, counting public finance and the private
finance it mobilises; no country has a fixed share.
At COP31: Focus turns to how to deliver. Developing countries are
pushing for the development of action plans to chart a pathway to
$300bn and for the Article 9 work programme to be included on the
formal agenda (with emphasis on Article 9.1 – which explicitly covers

Adaptation finance
COP31 focus: embedding and tracking the tripling pledge
Context: COP30 called for adaptation finance to at least triple by
2035, yet this was relatively vague; it did not specify a base year or a
target figure (some have suggested $120bn, i.e. 3x the 2025 goal).
At COP31: Parties need to resolve disagreement on whether to
anchor the pledge in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) and put a
finer point on the agreed Belém Adaptation Indicators that will track

it, after disagreement stalled the June Bonn talks.
Loss and Damage Fund
COP31 focus: guidance, replenishment and access
Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.
At COP31: Focus will be on its long-term funding strategy, with the
first replenishment cycle set to begin in 2027. $342m made available
in the first funding window, which offers $5–20m grants with at least

half reserved for SIDS and LDCs; but the voluntary nature of
payments, disputes over who pays and sensitivity around
“compensation” make major new pledges in Antalya unlikely.

### Page 8

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (1  o f 2 )
Adaptation will be one of the most important, and more divisive, negotiating topics
Adaptation is also set to be on the formal negotiating agenda at COP31. UN warnings that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable (at least temporarily), have
dampened morale for climate activists. As it relates to the COP agenda on adaptation, parties appear deadlocked on whether the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 should be

included in the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) text. This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal to investors the scale and timing of future public and blended finance flows.
The role of adaptation in the COP process
The Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA) was established under the Paris Agreement to enhance
adaptive capacity, strengthen resilience and reduce vulnerability to climate change. At COP30
last year, countries adopted 59 indicators (still being refined) to measure progress against the

goal. They also agreed a goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035. The pledge was vague: it set
neither a concrete figure nor a baseline year (a potential annual target of $120bn would be
triple the previous goal of c.$40bn by 2025).
Why GGA talks were deadlocked at the June climate meetings
Finance was the main sticking point at the June Bonn meetings. Developing countries wanted
the COP30 goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 referenced in the GGA text; countries

including Canada, Norway and Japan opposed. Parties also split on whether the indicators task
force should be expert- or party-led, so no GGA text was agreed and the decision passes to
COP31. Moving the Adaptation Fund to serve the Paris Agreement exclusively (funded by a 5%
share of proceeds from Article 6.4 credits) stalled in Bonn over the composition of its board;
talks will resume at COP31.
What is needed at COP31 to unlock adaptation progress

Adaptation is on the provisional COP31 agenda, including the tripling adaptation finance goal
(though this may get blocked). Some parties want it explicitly referenced in the final COP31
GGA decision; this should become clearer when the agenda is adopted as COP31 opens on 9
Nov. At issue: the tripling pledge still lacks a baseline and named contributors. At COP30,
developed nations resisted new finance obligations, while the EU and many others pushed for

a roadmap to transition away from fossil fuels, which a group of mostly oil- and gas-producing
countries blocked. The result was a looser tripling goal and no agreed fossil-fuel roadmap.
Developing countries are unlikely to give ground, as adaptation relies far more on public
funding than mitigation, so a repeat of COP30’s finance vs. mitigation debate is plausible.
Sources: United Nations, UNEP, Carbon Brief, European Parliament (COP30 outcomes briefing, December 2025)

### Page 9

TO P I C  3 | AD AP TATI O N  &  R ES I L I EN C E (2  o f 2 )
The EU’s proposed Climate Resilience Law plans for a 3°C world, while reiterating commitment to net zero 2050
The EU Commission’s recently proposed Climate Resilience Law would set a reference global warming trajectory of 3°C, providing a benchmark for member states’ adaptation planning. The EU
states it remains committed to reaching net zero by 2050, but some at COP will likely view the proposal as accepting overshoot. With the EU also pointing to an estimated need for EUR70bn in

annual adaptation investment, this provides an interesting backdrop as COP31 seeks to resume adaptation negotiations which stalled earlier this year, primarily due to finance.
What the Commission proposed
On 27 October the Commission presented its EU Framework for Climate Resilience, anchored
by a proposed European Climate Resilience Law. With current global policy commitments
pointing to around 2°C of warming by 2050 and 3°C by 2100, the law would set a common

reference trajectory of 1.5°C in 2030, 2°C in 2050 and 3°C in 2100, with this regarded as the
“baseline assumption”. Member states would be required to use it in national climate risk
assessments and adopt a single national resilience plan by end-2030.
Why it matters: the EU is planning for overshoot
The EU highlighted warnings that overshoot of the 1.5°C goal is now “unavoidable” (even if just
temporarily) as necessitating policy action, even as the EU says it is still committed to climate

neutrality by 2050. The Commission links the plan to Europe’s hottest summer on record and
estimates annual economic losses from climate extremes of €50bn over the past four years,
adding that output losses caused by climate change go far beyond this. It says EU adaptation
needs are ~€70bn a year through to 2050, with Europe warming at 2x the global rate.
What it means for COP31
The proposal demonstrates that warnings on the implications of a significant overshoot of the

Paris Agreement global warming limits are moving from scientific theory to real government
policy. While the opening line of the new EU framework emphasized it remains committed to
reaching net zero by 2050, some may view a 3°C planning baseline as accepting overshoot.
The headline €70bn annual EU adaptation finance requirement also provides an interesting
backdrop heading into Global Goal on Adaptation talks given they are stalled largely due to

disagreement over explicit links to finance in the GGA text. For context, the UNEP estimates
annual adaptation needs for developing countries of $310–365bn by 2035. Ultimately, we
expect adaptation focus to continue growing as warming impacts are increasingly felt and
societal calls for relief grow louder. Corporates will need to consider potential strategic
impacts, while some investors have already begun raising their baseline warming forecasts.

### Page 10

TO P I C  4 | EL EC TR I F I C ATI O N
Türkiye is championing a voluntary pledge to lift electrification from 23% to 35% by 2035
Electrification is expected to be at the centre of this “implementation COP”, targeting a rise in electricity’s share of global final energy use from 23% to 35% by 2035. We expect support for this
action given it is being positioned alongside energy security, but we also expect discussion and debate over how it is delivered (e.g., the role of renewables, nuclear and natural gas), alongside

concerns over clean tech dependency in supply chains. China and the EU have expressed support but not yet committed.
Some may brand this the electrification COP
The Action Agenda pledge aims to raise electricity's share of global final energy from 23%
today to 35% by 2035. The pledge was launched in Bonn this past June and formalised as the
"35-by-35 Global Electrification Pledge" in New York on 21 September.
China and the EU have signaled support, but have not yet signed the pledge

Electrification fits modern day energy-security goals making it a viable target for this year’s
COP. China’s cleantech dominance means it has a further incentive to support initiatives that
seek to increase the pace of global electrification. It also aligns closely with China's existing
development trajectory. While the 35% goal is based on Paris-aligned pathways supported by
IEA and IRENA analysis, the numerical target itself does not require the additional electricity to

come from non fossil fuel sources. This has invited some criticism, though it may make it
easier for some nations to sign the non-binding pledge.
Alignment with the voluntary renewables pledge
Recall that COP28 called for tripling global renewable power capacity by 2030 in COP28’s
formal Global Stocktake decision, equivalent to around 11.2 TW. IRENA’s September 2026
assessment puts capacity at 5.15 TW at end-2025. Record additions of 693 GW in 2025 remain

below the 1,200 GW needed annually in 2026–30. The 35-by-35 pledge complements this
supply goal by shifting transport, buildings and industry towards electricity, enabling
renewable power to replace fossil fuels. Its emissions benefit still depends on cleaner
generation.
We view the goal positively, but delivery needs grid enhancements.
IRENA counts ~2,500 GW of renewable, storage and large-load projects awaiting connection
and says annual investment to strengthen grid infrastructure and enhance system flexibility

must rise from ~$500bn in 2025 to around $1tn through 2035, and $1.2tn thereafter. That
said, signatories signal support, but domestic delivery plans will determine investment.
Sources: UNFCCC, COP31 Presidency, IEA, IRENA

The first conference pinpointed 5 pathways for the fossil fuel transition process
TO P I C  5   |   F O S S I L  F U EL S
The fossil fuel transition roadmap track is growing outside the formal COP process
COP30 failed to agree a fossil fuel transition roadmap, despite support from more than 80 countries. Brazil’s COP30 presidency is therefore developing a roadmap outside formal UN
negotiations to be presented ahead of COP31. Separately, Colombia and the Netherlands convened a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 countries in Santa Marta in April 2026 to explore practical

steps towards a fossil fuel phase-out. This parallel forum allows willing countries to cooperate without needing consensus from all COP parties.
Sources: Carbon Brief, Santa Marta conference report

TO P I C  6 | TR AD E
Trade could resurface at COP31 as a challenge to collective agreement; talks have been requested
Trade tensions could feature at COP31 as governments weigh climate ambition against industrial competitiveness. Developing countries fear that measures such as the CBAM disadvantage

their exporters, while the EU says they prevent carbon leakage. COP30 agreed three annual dialogues on trade's role in climate cooperation (no binding outcome), creating for the first time a
formal space for trade discussions in the UNFCCC process, but The African Group and others would like it on the formal COP31 agenda.
Do trade discussions belong at COP?
Developing countries have long pushed back against climate-related trade measures such as
the EU's Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) and Deforestation Regulation (EUDR).

They argue such measures are protectionism disguised as climate policy and
disproportionately impact poorer countries. Developed countries view such measures as
necessary to level the playing field for domestic firms in cases where foreign competitors face
lower environment-related compliance costs relative to domestic industries, while also arguing
that COP is not the appropriate forum to discuss trade matters. Despite this, a European
Parliament briefing noted trade is a point of “exposure” for the EU at COP31.

COP30’s agreement on trade dialogue was notable, but it may not be enough
COP30 agreed three annual climate-trade dialogues at the mid-year sessions in 2026-28, with
a high-level event in 2028. The first, in Bonn (13 June), was calmer than expected but exposed
the divide: developing countries argued that climate-linked trade measures such as CBAM
raise costs and restrict market access and called for impact assessments within the UNFCCC,
while the EU defended them as responses to environmental externalities. With trade also

raised in other Bonn rooms, it is far from clear the dialogue will contain the issue in Antalya.
The African Group of negotiators has requested a Trade dialogue on the formal agenda.
China has already said it intends to use COP31 to oppose cleantech trade barriers, with its vice
minister of the Ministry of Ecology and Environment saying the event should discuss “in depth”
the “new problem of unilateralism and protectionism undermining global collective efforts” on

climate. The African Group is also pushing for trade to be on the formal agenda. Ultimately,
trade talks could prove more consequential at COP31 than in previous years given inflationary
pressures and concerns over business competitiveness, but it is hard to assess the potential
impact other than possibly causing delays in agreement. However, even if the impact is
limited, the issue could shape negotiations elsewhere outside of the COP process.
Sources: European Parliament, Carbon Brief, Reuters

### Page 13

TOP I C 7 | G EO P O L I TI CS  & MU L TI L ATER AL I S M (1  o f 2 )
Multilateralism is at a crossroads
Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus among nearly 200 nations harder, encouraging “coalitions of the willing” (outside of the formal COP
negotiations) to pursue more ambitious climate goals. COP31’s success or failure in securing multilateral agreement or credible voluntary pledges could shape future COPs by showing which

approaches deliver progress. The US retreat from the Paris Agreement will also continue to have far-reaching implications for climate finance. Overall, the EU and China will be expected to lead.
The consensus agreement principle under strain
The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) provides the
multilateral framework for COP, the forum where countries negotiate collective climate
decisions. The process is anchored by the view that all countries must act on climate change,

but the extent of their efforts depends on national circumstances. This puts the onus on
developed countries to lead. The question is whether this principle still holds against a more
fragmented backdrop and transactional diplomatic context as national budgets prioritize
security and business competitiveness. With the COP process still separating developed and
developing countries by their 1992 UNFCCC classifications, developed countries are continuing

to push for wealthier developing nations such as China and Saudi Arabia to play a greater role
on finance. This has been a major point of contention at recent summits.
The US stepping back has far-reaching implications
The US skipped COP30 and left the Paris Agreement. It will formally exit the UNFCCC in
February 2027. This retreat has weighed on broader climate-finance decisions, both from a
monetary sense and through the loss of negotiating power. While cities, states and businesses

from the US continue to support COP efforts (e.g., groups like America Is All In), the federal
administration’s absence has put more pressure on the EU and China to lead.
What does an evolving process look like?
Because formal COP decisions require consensus (or lack of objection), progress often stalls or
moves slowly. COP30 saw Brazil move items where there was strong, but not unanimous,
support such as the fossil fuel roadmap, to separate streams outside the formal COP process.

Countries disappointed with the lack of stronger mitigation action also launched a separate
summit on transitioning away from fossil fuels, held this past April in Colombia. While COP is
the flagship global forum, these actions demonstrate a shift towards “coalitions of the willing”
for issues where global agreement is not possible.
Sources: EU Council, EU Parliament, Carbon Brief, UN, WRI

TO P I C  7 | G EO P O L I TI C S  &  MU L TI L ATER AL I S M (2  o f 2 )
Much will rest on the approach of the EU and China

With the US absent, the EU and China are the two actors with the greatest diplomatic and economic leverage with which to broker compromise agreements between the different factions at
COP31. This will require careful diplomacy, with their interests not fully aligned in some areas; discussions around trade and climate finance may be particularly challenging. At the same time,
both have reiterated their continued support of climate action and multilateral decision making; a successful COP outcome would back up these commitments.
